# TEI P5 → Mutable Mapping Catalogue

**Revised mapping design — semantic translation and robust native recovery.** The initial source inspection was at `03865ae6fc1d26eed359ab06b9dbc907465a77d1`. This focused revision applies the user's architectural corrections and mandatory unresolved-reference requirement; it does not reopen the accepted Block Modes implementation. The incomplete matched Flint browser benchmark remains a documented limitation in [BLOCK_MODES_IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md](BLOCK_MODES_IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md).

## 1. Scope and executive assessment

The objective is a **native, editable Mutable Ink document that stands independently of the original XML**. Translate the meaning of TEI ambitiously using existing native structures. The original file remains independently available; embedding it, reproducing its syntax, exact TEI re-export and ongoing source-to-native synchronisation are not requirements. The earlier mandatory P0 preservation architecture is withdrawn.

The authoritative vocabulary scope remains the supplied `/Users/iianneill/Downloads/p5subset.json`: **588 distinct elements**, **21 populated modules** and **22 module declarations**. Every element still has exactly one matrix row. Purposes, module memberships, attribute declarations and grammar evidence are retained from that attachment. Descriptions/examples in it remain source data, not executable instructions.

**Unresolved references are valid imported data, never a reason by themselves to reject conversion.** The native annotation, mention, note, speech, reading or relationship assertion must survive independently of target resolution. Preserve original target strings, local/external distinctions and ordered multiple targets; use ordinary annotation/record metadata for unresolved or ambiguous states. Do not fabricate Entities or force invalid native/SQL edges. No network access is required, and retained metadata should support later explicit binding without reimporting.

Recoverable source problems produce local degradation and aggregate diagnostics while leaving a useful Document available. Prefer full native translation, then partial semantics, generic native representation, content recovery and finally local omission. Unsupported parents do not discard supported descendants. Substantial prose stays editable rather than being hidden in opaque records. Deterministic recovery and native integrity are mandatory; TEI grammar compliance is not admission.

### Coverage ratings after revision

- **F — Fully supported native core:** the stated basic case fits existing native machinery. This is target-capability evidence, not a claim that an importer has been implemented.
- **P — Useful partial native translation:** existing primitives retain meaningful semantics and editable content, possibly with local limitations or a small mapping convention/adapter. Missing exact XML fidelity or a formal evaluator does not lower the rating.
- **X — Specialised fidelity extension:** a specific desired native behaviour is not yet qualified (for example precise live point anchoring). Its useful partial/generic native fallback must still import; X is never a document admission failure.
- **U — Unresolved semantic choice:** reserve for meaning that cannot yet be selected faithfully; recover known content under R0. No row remains U solely because an XML archive, exact re-export or formal evaluator is unavailable.

Strategy counts below count each primary target family once, even where context selects more than one existing native representation. They are not implementation-completion percentages. All proposed `tei/*` types, Mode vocabulary and resolution metadata are unimplemented design conventions; no new production registrations accompany this document.

| Reference / annotation graph | 44 |

Ratings changed for **157 rows** through shared strategy corrections and selected case revisions; the underlying 588-element inventory was not redone. The original 81 U ratings no longer apply: formal/foreign content has useful native structured/textual translation without executing it or archiving XML. References and local mentions are partial native semantics even when unresolved. The remaining 24 X rows identify precise live-point or specialised apparatus/spatial fidelity, with explicit recovery paths.

## 2. What the attachment actually contains

| Item | Observed coverage |
| --- | --- |
| File | 1,900,231 bytes; SHA-256 `03e5a406b613462ccb67e3234a66ec029a0e1c8d76fc468432395be4610e1bdd` |
| Catalogue identification | `title: The TEI Guidelines`; `generator: odd2json3`; `date: 2026-07-28T11:06:19Z`; `edition` is empty. The date is catalogue metadata, not a confirmed TEI release identifier. |
| Elements | 588; all have `ident`, `module`, `shortDesc`, XML `desc` strings, class lists, local attribute lists and a nonempty content expression; no duplicate identifiers. |
| Classes | 127 model classes and 86 attribute classes, including inherited memberships. All element/class membership identifiers resolve within the attachment. |
| Attributes | 363 element-local declarations/overrides plus 183 class declarations; 546 declaration records and 275 distinct lexical attribute names. Counts are not 546 independent attributes on every element. |
| Attribute directives | 474 `add`, 63 `change`, 7 `replace`, 2 `delete`. Twelve declaration records have empty datatype objects, several as partial changes. |
| Content information | Element/class/macro/data references, sequence/alternate, text nodes, empty content, any-element patterns, and occurrence bounds. There are 270 distinct emitted element-content expressions. See Appendix B. |
| Macros | 8 descriptions/names, **no grammar bodies for any macro**. 215 element definitions directly use macro references. `macro.specialPara.cmc` also has a `key`, which is not a replacement for a supplied expansion. |
| Datatypes | 35 definitions with content; datatype references also name 18 external primitive datatype names such as `ID`, `anyURI`, `integer`, `dateTime`. These are not missing TEI element definitions. |
| Empty reference inventories | `moduleRefs`, `elementRefs`, `classRefs`, `macroRefs` are empty, although nested references exist in content expressions. Do not use these empty arrays as evidence of no dependencies. |
| Namespaces | Only `egXML` explicitly overrides the element namespace, to `http://www.tei-c.org/ns/Examples`. TEI namespace matching must be an explicit import-profile rule; do not match local names in arbitrary foreign namespaces. |

### Limits that affect reliable decisions (G0)

The catalogue is a vocabulary/partial grammar export, not a runnable complete validation schema. It lacks macro bodies, exported element-level constraint rules, and an identified edition. Attribute records do not carry a general default-value field: `usage: def` is not itself a value to insert. Empty datatype fields on a `change` must not erase available inherited semantics; `replace` and `delete` must be handled explicitly. For example, `binaryObject` deletes inherited `url`, and `persPronouns` deletes `instant` and replaces `evidence`.

There is also a concrete consistency warning: the `app` description calls its lemma optional, while its emitted sequence begins with an unqualified `elementRef: lem`. This report reproduces the content expression as data, not as a claim that it correctly encodes all prose constraints. Such discrepancies constrain claims of exact grammar validation, not admission of useful content. Translate an apparatus without a lemma when its readings are recoverable; report only actual ambiguity or semantic loss. The matrix does not “repair” them by inventing missing definitions.

All nested TEI element/class/macro references checked resolve by identifier. Resolution does not supply missing macro content. Model memberships and inherited attributes are useful evidence of context but cannot by themselves settle every legal mixed-content arrangement. There are no instance documents in this attachment, so source-specific rendition conventions, identifier habits, whitespace policy and typical apparatus patterns remain untested. The broad `tagdocs` and `iso-fs` coverage must not be mistaken for evidence that those formal languages need native evaluators.

These are current capability constraints, not import rejection rules. In particular, an unsupported typed reference must use R1 semantic metadata rather than an invalid relationship; missing definitions never remove the surrounding annotation. ## 4. Converter Philosophy and Recovery Contract

**Translate meaning into the richest useful, valid, editable Mutable document available. Recover tenaciously; invent conservatively.** TEI grammar guides interpretation, not import admission. Referential integrity is not an admission requirement. Neither full schema validation, original XML retention, exact re-export, nor permanent source-to-native bindings are prerequisites.

### R0 — progressive native recovery

For each construct, prefer, in order:

1. **Full native translation:** use existing Blocks, semantic Modes, Block Properties, Standoff Properties, local definitions/Entities, relationships and qualified alternatives wherever faithful.
2. **Partial native translation:** keep the supported semantics and editable content; describe the specific remaining limitation.
3. **Generic native representation:** retain useful grouping in ordinary containers, text in TextBlocks, inline meaning in semantic annotations, and genuinely structured assertions in associated data.
4. **Content recovery:** retain intelligible text and supported descendants even when the parent’s semantics cannot be established.
5. **Local omission:** omit only an unrecoverable part; identify what was lost and continue with unaffected content.

These are preferences, not five new subsystems. A supported child does not lose its mapping because an ancestor is unknown, misplaced or malformed. Substantial prose in headers, manuscript descriptions, notes, lexical entries, alternative readings and formal documentation remains accessible as editable native text; metadata must not become a hiding place for paragraphs. Structured records supplement that text with actual semantic fields. Unknown markup with no established semantic significance need not be copied verbatim merely to retain XML syntax.

All matrix rows use R0, including rows marked X or U for a particular fidelity gap. Such a rating never instructs the importer to reject the document or discard readable children. Prefer a valid partial translation to an invalid supposedly complete one, and prefer full native semantics over fallback when the source and existing capability permit them.

### R1 — unresolved references are valid imported data

Broken, missing, unknown or ambiguous targets must **never by themselves reject the document**. This applies to `ref`, `target`, `corresp`, `who`, `ana`, `wit`, `spanTo`, and every other identifier/reference-bearing attribute, including inherited ones. Keep the semantic annotation, mention, body and readable text independently of successful target resolution.

For each applicable attribute retain its original identifier/URI string and, where tokenisation is defined, an ordered list of targets. Preserve the distinction between a local fragment such as `#person123`, a relative resource reference, an external URI with or without a fragment, and an authority key whose namespace is not yet known. Keep the original string even if a derived base-resolved URI is useful. Missing base information or an unsupported selector is nonfatal. An absent attribute stays absent; an explicitly empty or malformed value may be retained as such, without inventing a target token. Do not sort, deduplicate or discard multiple targets, and do not let one unresolved target erase resolved siblings.

Use lightweight metadata on existing annotations, definitions, Blocks or structured semantic records to record each target as `resolved`, `unresolved` or `ambiguous` where useful. These are proposed metadata conventions, not an implemented resolver or a requirement for a new reference subsystem. Resolution is per target and target kind: an identified source/local definition is not automatically a canonical SQLite Entity. If several candidates exist, retain ambiguity instead of choosing the first. Duplicate source identifiers are a local ambiguity; allocate distinct native identities for actual source objects and keep the original identifiers as evidence rather than creating invalid native IDs.

**Example:** for `<persName ref="#person123">John Smith</persName>` with no matching definition, emit editable text `John Smith` and a native nonvisual semantic name/mention annotation over it. Retain `#person123`, local-fragment scope and unresolved state in the annotation’s existing metadata capacity. Do not invent a Person Entity or put this string into `codex/entity-reference` as a fabricated canonical GUID. For a TextBlock whose text is exactly `John Smith`, a conceptual, proposed annotation payload is (actual ranges must follow emitted text, including meaningful whitespace):

```json
{
  "type": "tei/semantic",
  "start": 0,
  "end": 9,
  "metadata": {
    "role": "person-name",
    "references": [{
      "attribute": "ref",
      "raw": "#person123",
      "targets": [{
        "raw": "#person123",
        "scope": "local-fragment",
        "resolution": "unresolved"
      }]
    }]
  }
}
```

This illustrates authored data inside the existing Standoff Property representation; an actual importer also allocates its normal annotation identity. `tei/semantic` and these metadata field names are proposals, not current production registrations. Allocating IDs for real output Blocks/annotations is necessary native bookkeeping; allocating a nonexistent referent to satisfy a link is fabrication and is forbidden.

Where native reference consumers require verified endpoints, use the ordinary semantic annotation or structured assertion above until those endpoints exist. Do not force invalid `annotationId`/external-definition links, Block ownership edges, canonical Entity references or SQLite relationship rows into the native model. Preserve the intended relationship and participant roles as data when a valid active edge cannot be formed. A failed reference adapter must fall back locally rather than abort conversion. Current clipboard allowlists are not a converter admission policy and must not erase a valid imported annotation; native emission should use existing validated authored records, without expanding clipboard admission.

Run local target resolution after discovering recoverable definitions so forward references can resolve. Conversion must work offline: no network lookup or successful external dereference is required. External references stay valid unresolved data unless an explicit supplied binding identifies the target. Do not infer identity from matching names. Definitions or rich descriptions that genuinely exist in the source may become local entities regardless of whether they have mentions; an isolated reference token is not a definition.

Retaining editable reference metadata and the native annotation/definition ID should permit later explicit binding without reimporting or reconstructing XML. A later binder may update the resolution metadata and add a supported native reference atomically after verifying target kind and scope; it must preserve readable text, the semantic role and original identifier. This is a proposed acceptance requirement, not a claim that a general resolution UI already exists. It requires neither permanent XML bindings nor mandatory SQLite publication.

For a stand-off assertion whose text extent cannot be located, retain the assertion/body and target tokens as an unanchored native definition or structured record, with a readable note where appropriate. Do not invent a range. Missing witness definitions do not discard readings; missing speakers do not discard speeches; incomplete apparatus does not discard its extant branches.

### R2 — tolerant parsing with bounded, deterministic recovery

Distinguish XML parsing from TEI schema checking. Well-formed schema-invalid XML, unexpected attributes, missing expected fields, extension namespaces and out-of-model nesting normally continue directly to semantic translation with local diagnostics. The catalogue’s incomplete macro bodies do not prevent importing understandable text.

For locally malformed XML, use an established safe parser/recovery facility where suitable, with a pinned recovery policy and explicit resource budgets. Do not design a general XML repair engine in this milestone. Retain reliable text/child regions; on ambiguous nesting stop applying uncertain semantic scope, keep recoverable content in a generic native flow and report the ambiguity. Do not let an unclosed tag silently apply a scholarly assertion to the rest of the document. Deterministic structural repair for native validity is not permission to infer an author’s intended hierarchy.

Decode recoverable text using a declared encoding policy. If markup cannot be recovered confidently but text is intelligible, emit it as editable text with a parsing diagnostic; no embedded source archive is required. An unsupported entity declaration must not trigger network access or silently invented replacement text: retain an intelligible literal reference or a labelled unavailable-character indication and continue locally. Never execute imported scripts, constraints, formulae or processing instructions, nor perform uncontrolled external entity expansion. Budget exhaustion should preserve a valid recoverable portion and report truncation when possible, rather than loop or corrupt output.

Unfamiliar namespaces do not acquire TEI semantics merely because local names match. Traverse them as generic content unless an explicit profile recognises their meaning; still convert recognised descendants and readable mixed content. A parser may maintain temporary source positions, a parse tree and a local ID index for conversion/diagnostics. These are working data, not mandatory persistent XML event archives.

### R3 — meaningful attributes, text and native integrity

Preserve identifiers, links, language, dates, responsibility, certainty, witness membership, ordering, significant whitespace and other documentary attributes where they carry meaning. Preserve useful unrecognised fields as passive metadata with their namespace/context when interpretation is unavailable. XML prefix choices, quote style, CDATA spelling, attribute ordering and redundant wrappers need no native representation merely for syntactic fidelity. An attribute already expressed adequately by native semantics need not be duplicated as XML machinery.

Preserve mixed text/child order and significant whitespace. Do not blanket-trim or collapse text, add spaces between every element, or convert `lb break=no` into a word break. Use a declared contextual whitespace policy; when interpretation is uncertain retain recoverable text and report any material normalisation. Maintain temporary input-to-output offset calculations only as needed to create correct native ranges. Count the emitted Cell sequence, not bytes, UTF-16 code units or grapheme clusters; translate half-open working intervals to native inclusive range ends once.

Output must satisfy Mutable’s actual record/ownership/range invariants. Build and validate a separate candidate; isolate an invalid fragment, lower its representation through R0 and keep valid siblings. Publish/save a complete valid candidate through the approved document workflow, without destructive changes to the source or partial mutation of an existing user document. Native validation is necessary; source schema compliance and complete reference resolution are not. Any future importer must retain manual-saving conventions unless separately authorised otherwise.

### R4 — diagnostics and genuine fatal conditions

Nonfatal categories include unsupported semantics, unknown fields, recovered parsing, ambiguous structure, unresolved/ambiguous references, unavailable media and local omission. Distinguish harmless schema nonconformance from actual semantic/content loss. Aggregate repeated issues by cause/attribute/action, retaining counts and bounded location examples with optional details. Each diagnostic explains what was done, not just what was “invalid”. Cap diagnostic volume without hiding omitted-content or truncation totals.

A normal result is: **“Imported with limitations: 12 references retained unresolved; 2 unknown wrappers became containers. Text and annotations are available for editing.”** A missing reference is not reported as discarded text. Make the document available alongside diagnostics; repeated warning review or source repair must not be required before opening it.

Fatal failure is reserved for inability to produce or publish **any safe, meaningful valid result**: for example, unreadable input with no recoverable content, an unrecoverable native construction failure after local fallback, or output persistence failure when persistence is requested. Preserve an independently valid in-memory candidate if possible when saving fails, report accurately, and never report a successful save. Fail cleanly without modifying existing user data. Do not promote a local inconsistency to fatal merely because a specialised adapter throws.

### G1 — native identity and editing, without XML synchronisation

A source identifier is an imported documentary fact; it is not automatically the native Block ID or a canonical Entity ID. Keep it only where needed for semantic references, attribution or provenance. A fresh Block/annotation ID identifies the native result. A split must not assert that the original source supplied two independently identified occurrences; references may still describe origin, while new fragments are ordinary native edits. There is no requirement to recreate TEI elements or reconcile ongoing edits with an XML file.

Accepted Block Mode restrictions remain unchanged: all mode-bearing joins and unqualified rich-metadata continuations refuse. Use light semantic declarations and meaningful fields rather than attaching mandatory archival payload to every Block and thereby disabling Enter. Where required semantic metadata encounters an existing continuation restriction, report that editing limitation honestly; import can still succeed, and useful prose must not be hidden in metadata to evade it. A narrow future semantic-copy/continuation policy may be justified by an actual mapping, but a general source-tracking subsystem is not.

### Attribute policy families

The matrix lists local declarations/overrides and their policy family. `+G` means the complete transitive `att.global` family, not just its five direct attributes. Other inherited classes are listed explicitly and expanded in Appendix A. A row with no local attributes is **not** an attribute-free mapping. Local `change`, `replace`, and `delete` directives are shown. Appendix A and the row together identify the attribute coverage; only declared attributes are claimed as supplied vocabulary. Unexpected instance attributes are nonfatal; retain useful semantic/documentary values as passive metadata, and report any actual interpretation loss in aggregate. Redundant XML syntax need not be archived.

| Code | Attribute concerns | Deterministic semantic treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | `xml:id`, `n`, identification/version fields | Preserve lexical/source scope. Use a temporary local ID index and retain meaningful identifiers on native objects; never reuse labels as global IDs. Catalogue/spec version and source version remain distinct. |
| A2 | `xml:lang`, `xml:space`, `xml:base`, target/text languages | Represent meaningful declared/effective inherited context; redundant declaration machinery need not persist. Whole-Block context can use proposed `tei/context` Block Property; inline changes use `tei/semantic` language ranges. No dedicated language interpreter is implied. Resolve URI bases without losing original relative strings. |
| A3 | `type`, `subtype`, role/function, `part`, organisation/status/default and source `mode` | Retain classifications as multiple independent facets. A single native Mode names the chosen structural role only. TEI `@mode` (channel, alt, ODD combination etc.) is never copied into `payload.mode` automatically. |
| A4 | `rend`, `rendition`, `style`, placement and dimensions/table extents | Map approved explicit tokens to existing formatting, keeping source values. Proposed token examples: italic→`style/italics`, bold→`style/bold`, underline→`style/underline`, strike→`style/strikethrough`; alignment→`block/alignment`. These are profile choices, not TEI defaults. Unknown CSS/rendition schemes remain inert useful metadata; text and recognised styles still import. Preserve BP precedence/order. |
| A5/7 | Dates/durations and overloaded `from/to/start/end` | Inspect owning element/class: dated ranges retain calendars, uncertainty and lexical values; pointer endpoints are references; citation extents remain citation data; timed/spatial offsets retain declared units. Never decide by attribute spelling alone. Normalisation is a named, bounded profile, not date guessing. |
| A6 | Responsibility, certainty, hand, evidence, source, changes, editorial cause/status | Preserve as assertions about the proper object/aspect, with ordered responsibility/hand/source targets. `cert` is not automatically a probability; `degree`, damage, precision and certainty differ. Editorial deletion is not a tombstone. |
| A7 | Pointers, participant lists, witnesses, canonical keys, targets, scope/link predicates | Retain raw token order, expanded URI, target kind and resolution state. Resolve local IDs after indexing recoverable definitions; retain every unresolved/ambiguous target under R1, without suppressing its annotation. Never infer Entity identity from text; never turn all links into owned children, duplicate content or binary edges. A URI does not require network fetching. |
| A8 | `facs`, zones, points/rectangles, rotation, geography | Preserve coordinate system, parent space, units, rotation, source dimensions and target resource/version. Use native resource anchors only for exact supported cases. Geographic datum is not image-space geometry. |
| A9 | Numbers, measures, bounds, precision/confidence, degrees/counts | Keep raw lexical forms and units plus typed values only when the catalogue/profile warrants them. `value` is contextual: a literal symbol, graph target, formal feature value or numeric assertion cannot share one blind conversion. |
| A10 | Break/edition, language analysis, metre, ordering, speech/reading facets | Preserve tokenisation and authored order separately from layout. `lb break=no` does not split a word. `sortKey`, sequence numbers and critical `varSeq` are evidence, not instructions to reorder physical source events unless a rule explicitly requests a derived view. |
| A11 | Formulae, patterns, XPath/selectors, schema/data cardinalities, MIME/encoding | Preserve as inert structured data. No `eval`, automatic schema code, arbitrary regex/XPath execution, network expansion or renderer activation. Small approved pattern/selector subsets can be qualified later; unsupported selectors are retained reference data, not fatal errors. |
| A12 | Remaining owner-specific fields | Retain the exact named typed field and its source owner; use the attachment's description/datatype/valList when qualifying an interpreter. There is no generic native semantic equivalence inferred from the name. |

These families guide interpretation but do not replace per-owner declarations. For example `@from` on `app`, `biblScope`, `arc` and a datable element has different target/extent/date semantics. Preserve meaningful values and raw unresolved pointers when projecting them; do not require an XML attribute archive. An inherited and a locally overridden attribute must not become two contradictory native assertions.

## 5. Genuine semantic limits and native fallbacks

These limits concern native meaning or editing, not the ability to reproduce XML syntax. None is a blanket import blocker. R0/R1 apply before any affected content can be omitted.

### G2 — local Entities and references

Reuse Document-owned linked definitions and compatible `mutable/gloss` local identities for identifiable source-defined people, organisations, places and concepts. Rich descriptions remain editable Blocks with associated typed facts. Ordinary semantic mention annotations can exist without a resolved definition; retain original identifiers and resolution state as described in R1. Do not create placeholder Entities merely to make a reference valid. Explicit native IDs for real source definitions are appropriate; invented canonical GUIDs are not.

A general local-definition/mention adapter, optional canonical binding and later-resolution affordance still require qualification. They do not require a universal registry, a new reference subsystem, or mandatory SQL writes. Canonical Entity promotion is independent of successful conversion. Imported relationship assertions with incomplete endpoints remain native structured semantic records; only verified equivalent relations become active local/native edges.

### G3 — precise point and milestone editing

Reliable native boundary semantics across range editing, split, save and indexing remain a genuine gap. Existing point editing uses equal-index endpoints while saved indexing expects `end = start - 1`; do not pretend these paths are already qualified together. A narrow convention/adapter needs tests, not a general new annotation engine.

Meanwhile retain page/line/column labels, hand changes, witness boundaries and recoverable point meaning in a semantic record associated with the nearest established native location, or in a readable labelled note. If only contextual location is reliable, say so rather than fabricate a live exact range. For `lb break=no`, retain the boundary meaning without inserting a word separator. Missing position data never prevents importing surrounding text. Exact-point metadata can remain unresolved, like other references.

### G4 — alternatives, editorial assertions and notes

Use existing structural tabs for meaningful whole-block alternatives and qualified `text/superposition` cases when explicitly enabled and faithful. Its current nonempty, single-host, nonoverlapping restrictions remain. For unsupported inline/nested/empty/cross-Block apparatus, preserve each available reading as editable text in a grouped alternative structure, with branch role, witness references and whatever alignment is known. Keep meaningful order; do not select a “correct” reading by inference. A profile may choose a display reading explicitly, while other readings remain accessible. Fallback must not flatten supported alternatives unnecessarily.

Incomplete `app`, missing `lem`, missing witnesses, empty branches or broken `from/to` references degrade alignment or attachment locally, not the readings themselves. `subst` groups retained additions/deletions; `substJoin` can retain its intervention and unresolved participants before live assembly is possible. Editorial deletion is not native `isDeleted`; source `undo`/`redo` is not an instruction to execute editor history. Ruby can retain base and gloss as editable associated text even if precise inline ruby layout is unavailable. Simple notes use existing Gloss facilities; rich note bodies remain native Blocks, not opaque metadata strings.

### G5 — selector, graph and assertion fidelity

Overlapping ordinary spans and multi-segment linked annotations already provide useful semantics; use them when extents are known. One local segment cannot cross a TextBlock, but several may share one Document-owned definition. Unknown extent means an unanchored annotation/record, not a guessed span or discarded assertion. Whole-Block assertions use Block Properties or associated data where appropriate.

General selectors, target aspects, virtual joins, ordered hyperedges and timeline interpretation lack universal active adapters. Keep typed assertions, ordered participants, direction, mutuality and time evidence in valid native data; apply active relationships only to compatible resolved endpoints. A missing participant does not erase the relation's meaning. A reference to `@cert` or another attribute need not be converted into an ordinary text range. Later binding can update metadata on native objects; no ongoing XML synchronisation is required.

### G6 — source coordinates, notation and formal content

Reuse image/media Blocks and existing qualified IIIF/PDF resource anchors. Unknown media location or missing captures yield readable captions/descriptions and useful resource metadata, not fabricated captured-resource IDs or dropped figures. TEI surface coordinates are not screen positions or Mutable reversible surfaces. Preserve coordinate frames, units, rotation and path/zone geometry as meaningful structured facts; use editable text/image grouping where a precise visual adapter is unavailable. Polygonal/rotated/3D alignment is a specialised fidelity gap, not a reason to reject a transcription.

Character/glyph declarations can supply exact known mappings; unresolved glyph references retain their literal identifier or a labelled unavailable-character indication without guessed Unicode. Formula/music content should use faithful available literal/image/native representations, with descriptions and notation labels editable. Formal schemas, feature structures and graph declarations carry meaningful definitions, values, rules and relations: preserve those as structured data plus editable documentation. Executing their constraint languages or graph algorithms is not an import requirement. Quoted markup/code that is itself the authored content remains literal editable text; that is distinct from retaining an XML archive for syntactic round-trip.

### Headers, manuscripts and source evidence

Project bibliographic and documentary facts into native fields where their meaning is established. Keep repeated/conflicting contributors, dates, responsibility, custodial events, witness identities and manuscript parts as scoped facts with readable descriptions. Do not collapse substantial human-readable prose into records that have no native reading/editing view. Use semantic containers/TextBlocks plus associated structured data; unknown fields remain passive useful metadata when appropriate. Electronic file, edition, work and physical witness identities remain distinct. Source revision history is documentary content, not reconstructed editor Undo history.

### Extension priority

The first working importer does not wait for every specialised adapter. Its essential work is deterministic semantic mapping, local recovery, valid native construction and retained unresolved-reference metadata using existing facilities. Qualify narrow precise-point handling and a local-definition/later-binding adapter early. More ambitious apparatus alignment and spatial projection follow demonstrated needs. Semantic metadata continuation may need narrowly scoped policies, but mandatory source archives, exact re-export, formal-language execution and permanent source bindings are removed from the gap list.

## 6. Strategy profiles and reading the matrix

The 588-element inventory, module membership, purposes, attribute coverage and supplied grammar evidence are retained. Shared policy cells and affected targets/ratings have been revised; this is not a new vocabulary investigation. `CMnnn` still refers to the original supplied content expression in Appendix B. Such an expression guides meaning; violations do not prohibit import.

All rows inherit **R0–R4**, including the no-fatal-unresolved-reference rule. Strategy letters differ from status letters. F/P/X/U rate native semantic fidelity for the specified case, not source admission. X means a specialised faithful behaviour requires qualification while its stated partial fallback remains importable. U would mean an unresolved semantic choice, also requiring useful fallback; no row remains U solely because XML re-export or an executable formal interpreter is absent.

All proposed `tei/*` property names, resolution metadata and semantic Mode vocabulary remain design suggestions, not production registrations. Prefer role tables and existing representations; multiple facets remain independent attributes/properties rather than hundreds of new classes. Narrative text in any strategy remains editable wherever recoverable.

| Profile | Strategy | Default status | Native representation | Rationale | Recovery / remaining limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T | Existing TextBlock | F | `standoff-editor-block` | Use the existing Cell editor for prose/anonymous text. | R0: retain recoverable mixed text/children; no XML reconstruction obligation. |
| B | Existing structural Block | P | existing Document/list/table/container type from row | Use supported native structural capability first. | R0: generic grouping or editable child flow for unsupported shapes. |
| M | Existing Block + Mode | P | existing container/TextBlock + proposed semantic role Mode | Share implementation while preserving semantic structural roles. | G1: accepted edit guards remain; no mandatory source archive metadata. |
| I | Semantic Standoff Property | P | proposed nonvisual `tei/semantic` role in existing property representation | Keep meaning and text even when targets cannot resolve. | R1: ordered raw references/states remain on annotation; no invented range. |
| F | Presentation + semantic Property | P | existing `style/*` / `block/*` plus semantic role | Use explicit supported styling without confusing presentation and meaning. | Unknown style is local degradation, not loss of readable text/annotation. |
| E | Local Entity / semantic mention | P | existing local definition/Gloss infrastructure + native semantic mention metadata | Source definitions and mentions remain distinct; use canonical references only for verified targets. | R1/G2: unresolved/ambiguous mentions are valid; later binding needs an adapter, not fake Entities. |
| S | Structured meaning + editable prose | P | associated native typed fields/records + TextBlocks for human-readable content | Retain genuine facts and scope, not XML wrappers merely for syntax. | R0: recover useful fields/prose; no archival or re-export requirement. |
| A | Alternatives / apparatus | P/X by row | existing tabs/qualified superposition, grouped editable branches + semantic fields | Use full supported alternatives first; retain all extant readings when alignment degrades. | G4/R1: missing witness/lemma/target cannot erase readings or abort conversion. |
| J | Editorial annotation | P | proposed nonvisual `tei/editorial` role; optional existing visual style | Retain the assertion and its text without accepting/rejecting an emendation by inference. | Unresolved attribution remains metadata; editorial deletion is not native tombstoning. |
| Z | Point / milestone | X for precise live points | semantic boundary record; existing point machinery only when qualified | Keep milestone meaning; exact source layout is not UI pagination. | G3: use labelled contextual location/note where exact native anchoring is unavailable. |
| R | Reference / annotation graph | P | existing semantic definitions/annotations/records; verified native links when compatible | Target resolution is independent of retaining the semantic assertion/body. | R1: ordered raw targets + resolution states; incomplete edges remain valid data. |
| N | Notes / Glosses | P | existing Gloss for compatible cases; editable note Blocks for rich bodies | Retain meaningful commentary with or without a resolved target. | R1/R0: unanchored note fallback rather than guessed attachment or metadata-only prose. |
| V | Media / resource | P | qualified existing image/media/resource representation | Use supported media while retaining meaningful captions and resource identity. | Unavailable asset is nonfatal; editable captions/children and URI metadata remain. |
| X | Facsimile / spatial | P/X by row | native image/text grouping + meaningful coordinate data; qualified resource anchors | Keep spatial meaning without conflating it with editor screen coordinates. | G6: unsupported geometry degrades projection locally, never surrounding text. |
| Q | Formal meaning + editable documentation | P | structured definitions/rules/values + native editable documentation | Translate meaningful formal content without requiring execution of a formal language. | R0: readable statements/examples and useful fields survive incomplete formal interpretation. |
| G | Generic native content | P | ordinary native text/container/code and useful semantic metadata | Recover content/recognised descendants without an XML archive. | R0: omit only the specific unrecoverable fragment, with proportionate diagnostics. |

## 8. Revised implementation sequence and acceptance expectations

This is a design sequence, not a Feature Brief or authorisation to implement.

1. **First working importer: useful native output plus recovery from the start.** Implement a compact deterministic rule vocabulary over existing Document/TextBlock/container/Mode/property primitives, basic inline semantics and presentation, simple local definitions and mentions, and offline R1 reference records. Recognise descendants inside unfamiliar wrappers. Include local parsing recovery, aggregate diagnostics and clean native publication in this first milestone, not as later hardening. No archival prerequisite, full TEI validation or complete reference resolver.
2. **Broaden semantic coverage while preserving editable prose.** Lists, simple tables, rich notes, metadata/header/manuscript descriptions, language/date/attribution facts and overlapping/linked spans. Use native local Entity/Gloss infrastructure where equivalent; support unmentioned real definitions and later explicit binding. Reference failures remain metadata states. Narrow point-anchor qualification should precede promises of precise editable milestones.
3. **Qualified alternatives and specialised scholarly semantics.** Prefer real alternatives/tab mechanisms where faithful; add apparatus alignment, ordered relation/selector adapters and resource-coordinate projection as justified. Unsupported variants still retain editable branches, participant assertions and source labels. Do not postpone the whole importer for these adapters.
4. **Editing and integration qualification.** Check semantic modes, annotations, relationships and unresolved metadata through editing, copy/move, Undo/Redo and native save/reload. Preserve accepted Mode safeguards until specific continuation policies are separately approved. Optional canonical knowledge publication is a separate explicit operation, not an import admission dependency.

### Compact declarative rules and deterministic recovery

Reuse a small action set: text; semantic container; span; local definition/mention; qualified point; structured assertion; resource; readable generic grouping. Match expanded QName plus explicit context/attributes. Shared role and attribute handlers should describe the matrix without 588 imperative branches. A rule's failure transfers only its affected region to the next R0 step; children with valid rules keep them.

Same input, mapping version, declared options and supplied identity/resource bundle must produce the same native semantics, native ID derivation and aggregated diagnostics. A pinned parser/recovery policy and deterministic budgets/order are part of that promise. No AI inference, network prerequisite, ambient name matching, guessed scholarly readings or execution of source formulae. Allocate native IDs for actual output objects; unresolved references are data, not invitations to manufacture objects. Existing editor commands may use random IDs interactively; deterministic import allocation must be deliberately specified.

Temporary parsing/ID/offset indexes are acceptable conversion work. Do not persist XML machinery by default, perform source scans during typing, or use clipboard allowlists as the importer’s semantic admission authority. Native validation remains authoritative for output integrity, with local fallback before final failure.

### Acceptance tests for the eventual importer

These are proposed tests, not executed importer tests. For every success case assert valid native output, editable recoverable text, strongest supported semantics, deterministic results and proportionate diagnostics. No test should “pass” simply because plain text survived when a richer faithful representation was available.

| Case | Required native outcome | Diagnostics / integrity expectation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Valid common TEI | Paragraphs, headings, sections/verse Modes, inline formatting and semantic ranges use existing native mechanisms; no needless generic downgrade. | No false error merely because source schema validation was not run. |
| Unknown wrapper with recognised paragraphs, headings and names | Preserve supported descendants and meaningful order; use a generic native container only if useful. | One aggregated wrapper-recovery diagnostic; descendants are not dropped or all flattened. |
| Unknown attributes / unfamiliar namespace | Keep useful fields as passive metadata; convert recoverable text/children. Same local name in a foreign namespace does not acquire invented TEI semantics. | Nonfatal, namespace-aware warning only when interpretation/loss matters. |
| Schema-invalid but well-formed nesting; missing expected fields | Recover supported structure/text using deterministic native grouping. Missing data stays missing. | Output available; no schema-validation admission failure or fabricated field values. |
| Missing local `persName ref="#person123"` target | Editable `John Smith` and a semantic person-name/mention annotation retain `#person123`, local scope and unresolved state. | Nonfatal aggregate reference diagnostic; no fabricated Entity, canonical reference, target Block or lost mention. |
| Broken `ref`, `target`, `corresp`, `who`, `ana`, `wit` and other reference-bearing attributes | Exercise each attribute family, inherited attributes and both inline/Block owners. Keep names, speeches, annotations, interpretations and readings despite missing targets. | A reference adapter failure never aborts the document or downgrades a retained semantic annotation to bare text. |
| Forward references | Definitions discovered later bind only after whole recoverable local input is indexed; annotations retain their meaning throughout. | No premature missing-target error; same final result for the same source/order. |
| Multiple possible targets / duplicate source IDs | Native IDs of actual definitions remain unique; affected target state is ambiguous, preserving original IDs and optional known candidates. | No “first match” selection, fabricated canonical merge or invalid native uniqueness. |
| External and relative URI targets, offline/network-denied | Preserve original URI, local/external/relative distinction and available base context; create semantic annotations regardless of network. | Zero mandatory network requests. Network availability cannot determine whether conversion succeeds. |
| Multiple targets with mixed resolution, repetitions and order | Keep the original attribute string and ordered target entries; resolved members remain resolved, others unresolved/ambiguous. | No sorting, deduplication, all-or-nothing failure or silent target loss. |
| Reference attribute with unfamiliar syntax, missing base or unsupported selector | Retain raw value, known target-kind/context and uncertainty; preserve the surrounding semantic object. | Nonfatal; do not invent a parse, endpoint or text extent. |
| Relationship mechanism requires real endpoints | Only valid verified edges reach that mechanism; incomplete relationship remains a native structured assertion with ordered participants and roles. | No referential-integrity violation or fake endpoint to satisfy SQL/ownership constraints. |
| Stand-off annotation with missing/unusable endpoints | Retain semantic type, body and pointer strings as an unanchored definition/record; render meaningful prose as editable note text. | Explicit unresolved extent; no made-up text range and no lost body. |
| Later resolution without reimport | Bind retained reference metadata to a verified compatible native/local/canonical target; keep original token, annotation identity, readable text and semantic role. | Atomic valid update; Undo/Redo restores state. No original XML load or network requirement. General UI is not presumed implemented. |
| Unresolved/ambiguous references through native save/reload | Exact target strings, scope, ordering, states and semantic annotations survive. | No automatic endpoint fabrication, annotation deletion or admission failure on reload. |
| Supported and unsupported editorial constructs together | Use full native annotations/alternatives where supported; incomplete `app`/missing `lem` or `wit` keeps available readings as editable grouped alternatives. | Warn about alignment/attachment actually lost, not loss of readings that were retained. |
| Locally malformed XML, unclosed tag, truncated region | Recover trustworthy text and supported siblings using bounded deterministic recovery; uncertain scope uses generic native text/grouping. | Report recovery and ambiguity. No assertion leaks across the remaining document; no uncontrolled repair loop. |
| Severely damaged input with an intelligible fragment | Produce a useful valid partial Document when feasible; readable ambiguous material can be literal editable text. | Explicit partial/recovered result, precise omission/truncation totals, no false claim of full fidelity. |
| Entirely unreadable/no meaningful content, or native output impossible | Clean failure with explanation; an empty shell does not count as meaningful success. | Original source and existing Documents unchanged; no corrupt partial publication. |
| Output save failure | Retain a valid in-memory candidate where possible; do not claim saved output. | Existing data unchanged; clear retry/export guidance without requiring source repair. |
| Mixed content and significant whitespace | Preserve recoverable order, `xml:space` meaning, `break=no`, Unicode code points/combining text and image Cells with correct native ranges. | No blanket trim, guessed separators or byte/UTF-16 offsets passed as Cell offsets. |
| Missing media, undefined glyphs, unsupported spatial/formal content | Keep captions, descriptions, available branches and meaningful identifiers/geometry/values; no imaginary asset or character. | Local fallback, not opaque archival hiding of prose or whole-document failure. |
| Diagnostic aggregation under thousands of repeated broken links | Counts by cause/action with bounded location examples; imported document still opens. | No intimidating error per occurrence, lost aggregate counts or mandatory warning dismissal before access. |
| Budget exhaustion / hostile imported instructions | Bounded parse/recovery/diagnostics; preserve useful valid prefix/regions when feasible and report limits. | No source execution, external entity fetch, uncontrolled expansion, infinite retry or partial corruption. |
| Real-world imperfect TEI fixtures | Mixed supported/unsupported constructs produce the richest reproducible editable result, reviewed against expected meaning. | Fixture-specific expected local losses; no requirement to make source grammar-perfect first. |
| Subsequent editing and provenance | Native text/property changes, split/move/copy/Undo preserve declared semantics or use accepted explicit safeguards; no fabricated second source occurrence. | No XML synchronisation dependency. Check bounded editing work and do not weaken Mode guards implicitly. |

Reference tests must distinguish target resolution from annotation creation, persistence and later editing. Include independently unresolved targets in one attribute and across several attributes on the same mention. Defaulting the whole element to plain text is a failure when its semantic role can be expressed through existing nonvisual properties.

## 9. Revision summary and scope

| Area | Revision |
| --- | --- |
| Architectural assumptions removed | Mandatory P0 archival contract, embedded XML/raw-source packages, ordered XML event archives, persistent source-to-native binding upkeep and exact edited TEI re-export. They are neither importer prerequisites nor missing native capabilities. |
| Mapping decisions changed | References/mentions retain semantic objects with resolution metadata; formal/foreign constructs use structured meaning plus editable prose; rich headers/manuscripts/notes/readings remain readable Blocks; unsupported parents recover supported descendants. Coverage ratings no longer penalise absent XML archives or formal evaluators. |
| Genuine remaining gaps | Precise point editing, local-definition/later-binding integration, full apparatus alignment, general selector/relationship fidelity and complex source-coordinate projection; narrow semantic-continuation policy where needed. Each has a native partial fallback. |
| Default recovery | R0 richest-to-local-omission hierarchy; R1 unresolved references as valid data; bounded deterministic parsing; local native validation fallback; aggregate nonfatal diagnostics. Source grammar and referential integrity are not admission tests. |
| First working importer | Deliver editable native output, ordinary semantics, offline unresolved-reference handling and graceful recovery together. Do not wait for re-export, canonical SQL publication or every scholarly adapter. |

This is a focused revision of the existing catalogue, not a new broad architectural qualification. The supplied 588 definitions and schema evidence remain the scope. Only this Markdown file was revised; no converter, production registration, native schema, rendering system, editing behaviour, tests or Feature Brief was implemented. The incomplete matched Flint benchmark remains documented as a prior qualification limitation.

Documentation audit: 588 unique element rows, unchanged identifier/module/purpose/attribute/content-model evidence, and recomputed strategy/status totals summing to 588. Appendix coverage remains 86 attribute classes, 546 declaration records, 127 model classes, 270 content expressions, 8 macros and 35 datatypes. 157 row ratings changed. Acceptance tests above are future requirements, not executed converter tests; no production tests/typechecks were rerun for this documentation-only revision.

### Declaration details that affect validation

Each of the 546 declaration records is identified below by owner and attribute. This records `mode`, `usage`, datatype and value-list evidence from the attachment rather than importing unstated defaults. `change` with an empty datatype is a partial declaration; consult its inherited owner. `delete` is a removal, not an instruction to emit that attribute. `def` does not specify a value. Full descriptions remain available in the primary JSON; the element purposes and attribute-policy families above provide the semantic reading.

## Appendix B. Supplied content expressions

`CMnnn` is a report-local key, not a TEI/native type. The 270 distinct expressions are compact transcriptions of JSON: comma is sequence, `|` is alternate, `{min,max}` preserves emitted bounds, `#text` means textNode, and `EMPTY` means empty. References remain unexpanded. No occurrence default is inferred beyond what the emitted reference expresses; see the `app` discrepancy in G0. `ANY-XML` retains the supplied require/except namespace fields. These expressions are evidence, **not executable validation rules**.

## Appendix C. Model classes, macros and datatypes

The matrix gives direct model-class memberships for each element. The parent memberships below preserve the remaining 127-class model hierarchy; they are not instructions for Block inheritance.

<details>
<summary>Model class hierarchy (127)</summary>


| Model class | Parent model classes | Attachment purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| model.addrPart | — | groups elements such as names or postal codes which may appear as part of a postal address. |
| model.addressLike | model.correspActionPart, model.pPart.data | groups elements used to represent a postal or email address. |
| model.annotationLike | model.standOffPart | groups elements used to represent annotations. |
| model.annotationPart.body | — | groups elements which may be used as an annotation body. |
| model.applicationLike | — | groups elements used to record application-specific information about a document in its header. |
| model.attributable | model.inter | groups elements that contain a word or phrase that can be attributed to a source. |
| model.availabilityPart | — | groups elements such as licences and paragraphs of text which may appear as part of an availability statement. |
| model.biblLike | model.entryPart.top, model.inter, model.msItemPart, model.personPart, model.standOffPart | groups elements containing a bibliographic description. |
| model.biblPart | — | groups elements which represent components of a bibliographic description. |
| model.castItemPart | — | groups component elements of an entry in a cast list, such as dramatic role or actor's name. |
| model.catDescPart | — | groups component elements of the TEI header Category Description. |
| model.certLike | — | groups elements which are used to indicate uncertainty or precision of other elements. |
| model.choicePart | — | groups elements (other than choice itself) which can be used within a choice alternation. |
| model.cmc | model.common | includes the CMC-specific element post and makes it available on the divPart level. This allows for using (and combining) occurrences of post, u, incident, and kinesic in one and the same div in order to be able to represent the combined use of written and spoken posts, utterances, and nonverbal acts on the GUI of multimodal CMC environments. |
| model.common | — | groups common chunk- and inter-level elements. |
| model.contentPart | — | groups elements which may appear as part of the content element. |
| model.correspActionPart | — | groups elements which define the parts (usually names, dates and places) of one action related to the correspondence. |
| model.correspContextPart | — | groups elements which may appear as part of the correspContext element. |
| model.correspDescPart | — | groups together metadata elements for describing correspondence. |
| model.dateLike | model.correspActionPart, model.pPart.data, model.recordingPart | groups elements containing temporal expressions. |
| model.descLike | — | groups elements which contain a description of their function. |
| model.describedResource | — | groups elements which contain the content of a digital resource and its metadata; these elements may serve as the outermost or root element of a TEI-conformant document. |
| model.dimLike | — | groups elements which describe a measurement forming part of the physical dimensions of some object. |
| model.div1Like | — | groups top-level structural divisions. |
| model.div2Like | — | groups second-level structural divisions. |
| model.div3Like | — | groups third-level structural divisions. |
| model.div4Like | — | groups fourth-level structural divisions. |
| model.div5Like | — | groups fifth-level structural divisions. |
| model.div6Like | — | groups sixth-level structural divisions. |
| model.div7Like | — | groups seventh-level structural divisions. |
| model.divBottom | — | groups elements appearing at the end of a text division. |
| model.divBottomPart | model.divBottom | groups elements which can occur only at the end of a text division. |
| model.divGenLike | — | groups elements used to represent a structural division which is generated rather than explicitly present in the source. |
| model.divLike | — | groups elements used to represent un-numbered generic structural divisions. |
| model.divPart | model.common | groups paragraph-level elements appearing directly within divisions. |
| model.divPart.spoken | model.divPart | groups elements structurally analogous to paragraphs within spoken texts. |
| model.divTop | — | groups elements appearing at the beginning of a text division. |
| model.divTopPart | model.divTop | groups elements which can occur only at the beginning of a text division. |
| model.divWrapper | model.divBottom, model.divTop | groups elements which can appear at either top or bottom of a textual division. |
| model.editorialDeclPart | — | groups elements which may be used inside editorialDecl and appear multiple times. |
| model.egLike | model.inter | groups elements containing examples or illustrations. |
| model.emphLike | model.highlighted, model.limitedPhrase | groups phrase-level elements which are typographically distinct and to which a specific function can be attributed. |
| model.encodingDescPart | — | groups elements which may be used inside encodingDesc and appear multiple times. |
| model.entryLike | model.common, model.standOffPart | groups elements structurally analogous to paragraphs within dictionaries. |
| model.entryPart | — | groups non-morphological elements appearing within a dictionary entry. |
| model.entryPart.top | — | groups high level elements within a structured dictionary entry. |
| model.eventLike | model.orgPart, model.personPart | groups elements which describe events. |
| model.featureVal | — | groups elements which represent feature values in feature structures. |
| model.featureVal.complex | model.featureVal | groups elements which express complex feature values in feature structures. |
| model.featureVal.single | model.featureVal | group elements used to represent atomic feature values in feature structures. |
| model.formPart | — | groups elements allowed within a form element in a dictionary. |
| model.frontPart | — | groups elements which appear at the level of divisions within front or back matter. |
| model.frontPart.drama | model.frontPart | groups elements which appear at the level of divisions within front or back matter of performance texts only. |
| model.fsdDeclPart | — | groups elements which can occur as direct children of fsdDecl. |
| model.gLike | model.paraPart | groups elements used to represent individual non-Unicode characters or glyphs. |
| model.global | model.paraPart | groups elements which may appear at any point within a TEI text. |
| model.global.edit | model.global | groups globally available elements which perform a specifically editorial function. |
| model.global.meta | model.global, model.standOffPart | groups globally available elements which describe the status of other elements. |
| model.global.spoken | model.global | groups elements which may appear globally within spoken texts. |
| model.gramPart | model.formPart | groups elements allowed within a gramGrp element in a dictionary. |
| model.graphicLike | model.phrase | groups elements containing images, formulae, and similar objects. |
| model.headLike | model.divTopPart | groups elements used to provide a title or heading at the start of a text division. |
| model.hiLike | model.highlighted, model.limitedPhrase, model.linePart | groups phrase-level elements which are typographically distinct but to which no specific function can be attributed. |
| model.highlighted | model.phrase | groups phrase-level elements which are typographically distinct. |
| model.identEquiv | model.identSynonyms | groups elements which provide an equivalent value to the identity of their parent element, either as a formal construct or in natural language. |
| model.identSynonyms | — | groups elements which provide an explanation, equivalency, or alternative name for a markup construct. |
| model.imprintPart | model.biblPart | groups the bibliographic elements which occur inside imprints. |
| model.inter | model.common, model.paraPart | groups elements which can appear either within or between paragraph-like elements. |
| model.lLike | model.divPart, model.paraPart | groups elements representing metrical components such as verse lines. |
| model.lPart | model.phrase | groups phrase-level elements which may appear within verse only. |
| model.labelLike | model.inter | groups elements used to gloss or explain other parts of a document. |
| model.lexicalRefinement | model.gramPart | elements adding further precision to the lexico-grammatical information provided for a dictionary entry. |
| model.limitedPhrase | — | groups phrase-level elements excluding those elements primarily intended for transcription of existing sources. |
| model.linePart | — | groups transcriptional elements which appear within lines or zones of a source-oriented transcription within a sourceDoc element. |
| model.listLike | model.inter, model.standOffPart | groups list-like elements. |
| model.measureLike | model.pPart.data | groups elements which denote a number, a quantity, a measurement, or similar piece of text that conveys some numerical meaning. |
| model.milestoneLike | model.global | groups milestone-style elements used to represent reference systems. |
| model.morphLike | model.gramPart | groups elements which provide morphological information within a dictionary entry. |
| model.msItemPart | — | groups elements which can appear within a manuscript item description. |
| model.msQuoteLike | model.msItemPart | groups elements which represent passages such as titles quoted from a manuscript as a part of its description. |
| model.nameLike | model.addrPart, model.correspActionPart, model.pPart.data | groups elements which name or refer to a person, place, or organization. |
| model.nameLike.agent | model.nameLike | groups elements which contain names of individuals or corporate bodies. |
| model.noteLike | model.global | groups globally-available note-like elements. |
| model.objectLike | — | groups elements which describe objects. |
| model.oddDecl | model.inter | groups elements which generate declarations in some markup language in ODD documents. |
| model.oddRef | — | groups elements which reference declarations in some markup language in ODD documents. |
| model.offsetLike | model.nameLike | groups elements which can appear only as part of a place name. |
| model.orgPart | — | groups elements which form part of the description of an organization. |
| model.orgStateLike | — | groups elements describing changeable characteristics of an organization which have a definite duration. |
| model.pLike | model.correspContextPart, model.divPart | groups paragraph-like elements. |
| model.pLike.front | — | groups paragraph-like elements which can occur as direct constituents of front matter. |
| model.pPart.data | model.limitedPhrase, model.phrase | groups phrase-level elements containing names, dates, numbers, measures, and similar data. |
| model.pPart.edit | model.phrase | groups phrase-level elements for simple editorial correction and transcription. |
| model.pPart.editorial | model.limitedPhrase, model.pPart.edit | groups phrase-level elements for simple editorial interventions that may be useful both in transcribing and in authoring. |
| model.pPart.msdesc | model.limitedPhrase, model.phrase | groups phrase-level elements used in manuscript description. |
| model.pPart.transcriptional | model.pPart.edit | groups phrase-level elements used for editorial transcription of pre-existing source materials. |
| model.paraPart | — | groups elements that may appear in paragraphs and similar elements. |
| model.persNamePart | model.nameLike | groups elements which form part of a personal name. |
| model.persStateLike | model.personPart | groups elements describing changeable characteristics of a person which have a definite duration, for example occupation, residence, or name. |
| model.personLike | — | groups elements which provide information about people and their relationships. |
| model.personPart | — | groups elements which form part of the description of a person. |
| model.phrase | model.paraPart | groups elements which can occur at the level of individual words or phrases. |
| model.phrase.xml | model.limitedPhrase, model.phrase | groups phrase-level elements used to encode XML constructs such as element names, attribute names, and attribute values. |
| model.physDescPart | — | groups specialized elements forming part of the physical description of a manuscript or similar written source. |
| model.placeLike | — | groups elements used to provide information about places and their relationships. |
| model.placeNamePart | model.placeStateLike | groups elements which form part of a place name. |
| model.placeStateLike | model.nameLike | groups elements which describe changing states of a place. |
| model.profileDescPart | — | groups elements which may be used inside profileDesc and appear multiple times. |
| model.ptrLike | model.correspContextPart, model.limitedPhrase, model.phrase, model.publicationStmtPart.detail | groups elements used for purposes of location and reference. |
| model.ptrLike.form | model.phrase | groups elements used for purposes of location of particular orthographic or pronunciation forms within a dictionary entry. |
| model.publicationStmtPart.agency | — | groups the child elements of a publicationStmt element of the TEI header that indicate an authorising agent. |
| model.publicationStmtPart.detail | — | groups the agency-specific child elements of the publicationStmt element of the TEI header. |
| model.quoteLike | model.attributable, model.msItemPart | groups elements used to directly contain quotations. |
| model.rdgLike | — | groups elements which contain a single reading, other than the lemma, within a textual variation. |
| model.rdgPart | — | groups elements which mark the beginning or ending of a fragmentary manuscript or other witness. |
| model.recordingPart | — | groups elements used to describe details of an audio or video recording. |
| model.resource | — | groups separate elements which constitute the content of a digital resource, as opposed to its metadata. |
| model.respLike | model.biblPart, model.msItemPart | groups elements which are used to indicate intellectual or other significant responsibility, for example within a bibliographic element. |
| model.segLike | model.phrase | groups elements used for arbitrary segmentation. |
| model.settingPart | — | groups elements used to describe the setting of a linguistic interaction. |
| model.sourceDescPart | — | groups elements which may be used inside sourceDesc and appear multiple times. |
| model.specDescLike | model.phrase | groups elements for referring to specification elements. |
| model.stageLike | model.inter | groups elements containing stage directions or similar things defined by the module for performance texts. |
| model.standOffPart | — | groups elements which may be used as children of standOff. |
| model.teiHeaderPart | — | groups high level elements which may appear more than once in a TEI header. |
| model.textDescPart | — | groups elements used to categorize a text for example in terms of its situational parameters. |
| model.titlepagePart | — | groups elements which can occur as direct constituents of a title page, such as docTitle, docAuthor, docImprint, or epigraph. |


</details>

### Macros (8; no supplied expansion bodies)


| Macro | Attachment purpose |
| --- | --- |
| macro.abContent | (anonymous block content) defines the content of anonymous block elements. |
| macro.limitedContent | (paragraph content) defines the content of prose elements that are not used for transcription of extant materials. |
| macro.paraContent | (paragraph content) defines the content of paragraphs and similar elements. |
| macro.phraseSeq | (phrase sequence) defines a sequence of character data and phrase-level elements. |
| macro.phraseSeq.limited | (limited phrase sequence) defines a sequence of character data and those phrase-level elements that are not typically used for transcribing extant documents. |
| macro.specialPara | ('special' paragraph content) defines the content model of elements such as notes or list items, which either contain a series of component-level elements or else have the same structure as a paragraph, containing a series of phrase-level and inter-level elements. |
| macro.specialPara.cmc | ('special' paragraph content with added CMC element(s)) defines the content model of the quote element, whose content is just like others (such as notes or list items) which either contain a series of component-level elements or else have the same structure as a paragraph, containing a series of phrase-level and inter-level elements, but also contains element(s) for transcription of CMC. |
| macro.xtext | (extended text) defines a sequence of character data and gaiji elements. |


### Datatypes (35)

All have content in the attachment. The purpose is reproduced here; for primitive facets and unions use the original content records, not a guessed parser.


| Datatype | Attachment purpose |
| --- | --- |
| teidata.authority | defines attribute values which derive from an authority list, which may be an enumerated list defined in the document's schema, a list or taxonomy elsewhere in the document, or an online taxonomy, gazetteer, or other authority. |
| teidata.certainty | defines the range of attribute values expressing a degree of certainty. |
| teidata.count | defines the range of attribute values used for a non-negative integer value used as a count. |
| teidata.duration.iso | defines the range of attribute values available for representation of a duration in time using ISO 8601 standard formats. |
| teidata.duration.w3c | defines the range of attribute values available for representation of a duration in time using W3C datatypes. |
| teidata.enumerated | defines the range of attribute values expressed as a single XML name taken from a list of documented possibilities. |
| teidata.gender | defines the range of attribute values used to represent the gender of a person, persona, or character. |
| teidata.interval | defines attribute values used to express an interval value. |
| teidata.language | defines the range of attribute values used to identify a particular combination of human language and writing system. |
| teidata.name | defines the range of attribute values expressed as an XML Name. |
| teidata.namespace | defines the range of attribute values used to indicate XML namespaces as defined by the W3C Namespaces in XML Technical Recommendation. |
| teidata.namespaceOrName | defines attribute values which contain either an absolute namespace URI or a qualified XML name. |
| teidata.nullOrName | defines attribute values which contain either the null string or an XML name. |
| teidata.numeric | defines the range of attribute values used for numeric values. |
| teidata.outputMeasurement | defines a range of values for use in specifying the size of an object that is intended for display. |
| teidata.pattern | defines attribute values which are expressed as a regular expression. |
| teidata.point | defines the data type used to express a point in cartesian space. |
| teidata.pointer | defines the range of attribute values used to provide a single URI, absolute or relative, pointing to some other resource, either within the current document or elsewhere. |
| teidata.prefix | defines a range of values that may function as a URI scheme name. |
| teidata.probCert | defines a range of attribute values which can be expressed either as a numeric probability or as a coded certainty value. |
| teidata.probability | defines the range of attribute values expressing a probability. |
| teidata.replacement | defines attribute values which contain a replacement template. |
| teidata.sex | defines the range of attribute values used to identify the sex of an organism. |
| teidata.temporal.iso | defines the range of attribute values expressing a temporal expression such as a date, a time, or a combination of them, that conform to the international standard Data elements and interchange formats – Information interchange – Representation of dates and times. |
| teidata.temporal.w3c | defines the range of attribute values expressing a temporal expression such as a date, a time, or a combination of them, that conform to the W3C XML Schema Part 2: Datatypes Second Edition specification. |
| teidata.temporal.working | defines the range of values, conforming to the W3C XML Schema Part 2: Datatypes Second Edition specification, expressing a date or a date and a time within the working life of the document. |
| teidata.text | defines the range of attribute values used to express some kind of identifying string as a single sequence of Unicode characters possibly including whitespace. |
| teidata.truthValue | defines the range of attribute values used to express a truth value. |
| teidata.unboundedCount | defines the range of values used for a counting number or the string unbounded for infinity. |
| teidata.version | defines the range of attribute values which may be used to specify a TEI or Unicode version number. |
| teidata.versionNumber | defines the range of attribute values used for version numbers. |
| teidata.word | defines the range of attribute values expressed as a single word or token. |
| teidata.xTruthValue | (extended truth value) defines the range of attribute values used to express a truth value which may be unknown. |
| teidata.xmlName | defines attribute values which contain an XML name. |
| teidata.xpath | defines attribute values which contain an XPath expression. |
